People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. So... that's why I'm here.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt like you're experiencing things that others don't seem to understand or aren't real, like hearing voices or having thoughts that feel foreign to you?\n\nPatient: I don't really know what other people hear. But... I guess. Sometimes. \n\nLike at work, by the mop closet... or coming out of the air vents. I'll hear a man talking. He just says my name, or... he tells me I ruined everything. It's not in my head. It sounds exactly like he's standing right next to me. \n\nBut... I don't really like talking about it. That's just what happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had thoughts or beliefs that other people don’t seem to understand or that turn out not to be true, like something you’re convinced is happening but others say it isn’t?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. People tell me I'm wrong about things. \n\nLike at work. The building people. I just know they marked me. They use the hallway lights... and they give these little looks to warn each other about me when I'm around. \n\nMy supervisor tried to say I was making it up. He even pulled up the security camera and showed me, said there was nothing on it. But... I'm still sure they were doing it. It doesn't really matter what the screen showed. \n\nI don't really want to talk about them, though. That's just what happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens when your thoughts or speech feel mixed up? Do you ever find it hard to organize your thoughts or feel like you're jumping from one idea to another very quickly?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. They jump around. \n\nIt's just... fast. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head all at once. I'll be thinking about the bills... and then it's the jail, or the mosque, or the cleaning chemicals at work. Just... before I can even catch one thought, it's already moved on to the next thing. \n\nI don't really know how to explain it better than that. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed changes in your mood, like feeling very down or very excited, that happen at the same time as these experiences you’ve been describing?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Yeah. They happen together. \n\nLike... there was a time I wasn't sleeping much. Maybe just a couple hours a night. I had all these big plans for the buildings... how to run them, how to fix everything. And... that's when I was hearing that voice from the vents. And seeing the hallway lights, with the warnings. It was all just going on right then. \n\nBut... I don't really want to get into all the plans and stuff. That's just how it was.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energetic, didn't need much sleep, or had big ideas or plans that others thought were unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I brought that up before. \n\nSometimes I just... I only sleep two, maybe three hours. I'll get up before dawn, and I feel fine. Fresh, even. I just start planning out all the work stuff. It's like... sleep is just something other people need to do. Not me. \n\nBut... I don't really have much else to say about it. That's just how it gets.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt extremely down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, lasting for days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. There are bad days. \n\nSometimes after morning prayer, I just... I sit on the edge of the bed. Everything just feels heavy. Empty. Like... there's no point in even cleaning myself up, or going outside. \n\nI just sit there. I don't really want to talk about it much more than that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so full of energy or ideas that it felt like you couldn’t slow down, even if others around you thought it was too much or not realistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. \n\nWhen it gets like that... I just can't sit still. I'll be at work and I just keep going. I'll clean the same stretch of hallway... over and over. Move all the chairs, put 'em back. Make lists of what needs doing. \n\nI just keep walking the floors. Even when my feet are aching, I just... I can't stop. \n\nThat's about it, really.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was extremely high or irritable, along with so much energy or racing thoughts that it was hard for you to function normally or others noticed something was off?\n\nPatient: Yeah... sometimes. \n\nI don't know, I just get... really up. Like I'm on top of everything. I'll be talking loud, laughing with the people in the building, feeling... powerful, I guess. \n\nBut then... if someone gets in my way or asks me a stupid question, I just... I snap. I'll yell at them over nothing. \n\nIt just happens. I don't really want to get into it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so energetic or excited that it lasted for days, not just a few hours, and during that time you were doing a lot more than usual or felt like you couldn’t slow down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose. \n\nWhen I get like that... I just start doing things. I'll call up guys I used to work with, tell 'em I can take on extra buildings to clean. Or I get these ideas to set up... community events and stuff. \n\nI stay up all night writing out these big schedules for it all. Even though... I never end up doing most of it later. \n\nI just get wrapped up in it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt extremely energetic or in a very high mood that lasted for at least a full week, not just a day or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah... longer than a week. \n\nIf you add it all up... all those times with the high energy, barely sleeping, making all those plans... it's a lot. Probably... about a year and eight months altogether. Just... total. \n\nI guess.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It doesn't really matter what the screen showed. \n\nI don't really want to talk about them, though. That's just what happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens when your thoughts or speech feel mixed up? Do you ever find it hard to organize your thoughts or feel like you're jumping from one idea to another very quickly?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. They jump around. \n\nIt's just... fast. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head all at once. I'll be thinking about the bills... and then it's the jail, or the mosque, or the cleaning chemicals at work. Just... before I can even catch one thought, it's already moved on to the next thing. \n\nI don't really know how to explain it better than that. That's just how it gets.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt extremely down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, lasting for days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. There are bad days. \n\nSometimes after morning prayer, I just... I sit on the edge of the bed. Everything just feels heavy. Empty. They use the hallway lights... and they give these little looks to warn each other about me when I'm around. \n\nMy supervisor tried to say I was making it up. He even pulled up the security camera and showed me, said there was nothing on it. But... I'm still sure they were doing it. It doesn't really matter what the screen showed. \n\nI don't really want to talk about them, though. That's just what happened.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe what happens when your thoughts or speech feel mixed up? Do you ever find it hard to organize your thoughts or feel like you're jumping from one idea to another very quickly?\n\nPatient: I guess... yeah. They jump around. \n\nIt's just... fast. Like a whole crowd talking inside my head all at once. I'll be thinking about the bills... and then it's the jail, or the mosque, or the cleaning chemicals at work. Just... before I can even catch one thought, it's already moved on to the next thing. \n\nI don't really know how to explain it better than that. It just happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed changes in your mood, like feeling very down or very excited, that happen at the same time as these experiences you’ve been describing?\n\nPatient: I guess so. Yeah. They happen together. \n\nLike... there was a time I wasn't sleeping much. Maybe just a couple hours a night. I had all these big plans for the buildings... how to run them, how to fix everything. And... that's when I was hearing that voice from the vents. And seeing the hallway lights, with the warnings. It was all just going on right then. \n\nBut... I don't really want to get into all the plans and stuff. That's just how it was.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt unusually energetic, didn't need much sleep, or had big ideas or plans that others thought were unrealistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I brought that up before. \n\nSometimes I just... I only sleep two, maybe three hours. I'll get up before dawn, and I feel fine. Fresh, even. I just start planning out all the work stuff. It's like... sleep is just something other people need to do. Not me. \n\nBut... I don't really have much else to say about it. That's just how it gets.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt extremely down, hopeless, or lost interest in things you usually enjoy, lasting for days or longer?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. There are bad days. \n\nSometimes after morning prayer, I just... I sit on the edge of the bed. Everything just feels heavy. Empty. Like... there's no point in even cleaning myself up, or going outside. \n\nI just sit there. I don't really want to talk about it much more than that.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so full of energy or ideas that it felt like you couldn’t slow down, even if others around you thought it was too much or not realistic?\n\nPatient: Yeah... I guess. \n\nWhen it gets like that... I just can't sit still. I'll be at work and I just keep going. I'll clean the same stretch of hallway... over and over. Move all the chairs, put 'em back. Make lists of what needs doing. \n\nI just keep walking the floors. Even when my feet are aching, I just... I can't stop. \n\nThat's about it, really.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was extremely high or irritable, along with so much energy or racing thoughts that it was hard for you to function normally or others noticed something was off?\n\nPatient: Yeah... sometimes. \n\nI don't know, I just get... really up. Like I'm on top of everything. I'll be talking loud, laughing with the people in the building, feeling... powerful, I guess. \n\nBut then... if someone gets in my way or asks me a stupid question, I just... I snap. I'll yell at them over nothing. \n\nIt just happens. I don't really want to get into it.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt so energetic or excited that it lasted for days, not just a few hours, and during that time you were doing a lot more than usual or felt like you couldn’t slow down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose. \n\nWhen I get like that... I just start doing things. I'll call up guys I used to work with, tell 'em I can take on extra buildings to clean. Or I get these ideas to set up... community events and stuff. \n\nI stay up all night writing out these big schedules for it all. Even though... I never end up doing most of it later. \n\nI just get wrapped up in it, I guess.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time when you felt extremely energetic or in a very high mood that lasted for at least a full week, not just a day or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah... longer than a week. \n\nIf you add it all up... all those times with the high energy, barely sleeping, making all those plans... it's a lot. Probably... about a year and eight months altogether. Just... total. \n\nI guess.\n\nDoctor: Was there a time when your mood was so high, irritable, or energized that it lasted for an entire week or more, not just a day or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I... I guess so. \n\nMost of the time since all this started, honestly... I've been in those stretches. Just... keyed up. Talking too much. \n\nI get these ideas... making these impossible plans to clean everything, everywhere. I just feel... driven. Like I can't slow down, no matter what. \n\nIt's just... most of the time.\n\nDoctor: Was there a time when your mood was so high, irritable, or energized that it lasted for at least a full week, not just a day or two?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess.
